By providing an upper bound for the noise term in the same way,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}a_{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{if }s\leq A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1},\\
\frac{a_{2}\eta_{e}s}{3CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}{(\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}^{k^{*}-1},\quad\text{if }s>A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1}\end{cases}

with high probability.

Thus,

\displaystyle{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\Big\{{\lvert{\kappa^{0}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\max\Big\{{\lvert{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},Q^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},g}\Big\}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

In contrast, the following inequality holds for
Q^{s}_{\text{I},g}
:

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{I},g}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}{(Q^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},g})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle+A_{3}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, by induction, we establish that
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert
is upper bounded by
Q^{s}_{\text{I},g}
for all
j\in\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
and
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

We also consider a similar argument for
j\notin\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
.

\displaystyle\lvert\kappa^{s+1}_{c,m,j}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\sum_{c\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}{\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j}})

\displaystyle\quad+i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}})\Big]+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{({\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{s}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\rvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\Big(\lvert{\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}{(1-{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2})}}\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\neq c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j})}\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}is_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({{w^{*}_{c}}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}+\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}is_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i}\Big\rvert\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{({\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\lvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\Big(k^{*}\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}}\rvert{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}+{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}}\rvert}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}

\displaystyle\quad+C{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{c^{\prime}\neq c,i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}}\rvert}\max_{c^{\prime}\neq c}{{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}}(\max_{c^{\prime}\neq c}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}\rvert}+\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert)

\displaystyle\quad+C{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{c^{\prime}\neq c,i}\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}}\rvert{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}(\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}}\rvert+\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert{({\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta}^{s}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\rvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}+(1+\frac{2}{3}a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max\big\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1},{(\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{k^{*}-1}}\big\}+\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\Big\}.

Since,
{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
c\neq c^{\prime}
,
{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
c
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
j\in[J]
, the term
{k^{*}}\max_{i}\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}}\rvert{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}
subsumes the remaining terms within the expression
\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}(\cdot)
with wight
\frac{1}{3}a_{2}
in the fourth inequality.
\frac{\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert{{(\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}
are also subsumed with weight
\frac{1}{3}a_{2}
by
\eta^{s}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
and
\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the fourth inequality.

By upper bounding the noise term in the same way, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}a_{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{if }s\leq A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1},\\
\frac{a_{2}\eta_{e}s}{3CM^{2}}k^{*}\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}{(\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}^{k^{*}-1},\quad\text{if }s>A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1}\end{cases}

with high probability.

Thus,

\displaystyle\max_{c\in[C]}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}\max\Big\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},R^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},c}\Big\}^{k^{*}-1}.

In contrast, the following inequality holds for
R^{s}_{\text{I},c}
:

\displaystyle R^{s+1}_{\text{I},c}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}{(R^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},c})}^{k^{*}-1}.

Therefore, by induction, we establish that
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert
is upper bounded by
R^{s}_{\text{I},c}
for all
j\notin\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
and
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

\displaystyle\lvert{\kappa^{s+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big(i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}-{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}{\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j}})

\displaystyle\quad+i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{({\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}})}^{2})\Big)+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert{({\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\rvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\Big(\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j})}^{i-1}({{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}-\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j}})}}\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}is_{c^{\prime}}\alpha_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{2})}\rvert\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}}\rvert{({\eta}^{t})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+\eta^{s}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\rvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\Big\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}+\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}\Big(C{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{c^{\prime},i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}}\rvert}\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c^{\prime},m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}(\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}\rvert}+\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert)

\displaystyle\quad+{k^{*}}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}\rvert}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}+C{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{c^{\prime},i}{\lvert{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert{({\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+\eta^{s}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s}}_{w_{m,j}}\Big\rvert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{A_{1}\eta_{e},\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}}\rvert}+(1+\frac{2}{3}a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}\rvert}\max\big\{{\lvert{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1},{(\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{k^{*}-1}}\big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s^{\prime}}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\Big\}.

Since,
{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
c\neq c^{\prime}
,
{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
c
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
j\in[J]
, the term
{k^{*}}\max_{i}\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}\rvert{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}
subsumes the remaining terms within the expression
\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}(\cdot)
with wight
\frac{1}{3}a_{2}
in the fourth inequality.
\frac{\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\rvert{{(\eta}^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{{({\eta}^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}
are also subsumed with weight
\frac{1}{3}a_{2}
by
\eta^{s}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
and
\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in the fourth inequality.

By upper bounding the noise term in the same way, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s^{\prime}}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}a_{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{if }s\leq A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1},\\
\frac{a_{2}\eta_{e}s}{3CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}\rvert}{(\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}^{k^{*}-1},\quad\text{if }s>A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1}\end{cases}

with high probability.

Thus,

\displaystyle{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\Big\{{\lvert{\kappa^{0}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\max\Big\{{\lvert{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},R^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},g}\Big\}^{k^{*}-1}

In contrast, the following inequality holds for
R^{s}_{\text{I},c}
:

\displaystyle R^{s+1}_{\text{I},g}

\displaystyle\leq(1+a_{2})\max\{\max_{c\neq c^{*}_{m}}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}+(1+a_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}{(R^{s^{\prime}}_{\text{I},g})}^{k^{*}-1}.

Therefore, by induction, we establish that
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert
is upper bounded by
R^{s}_{\text{I},g}
for all
j\notin\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
and
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.
Finally, we consolidate the auxiliary sequences
Q^{s}_{\text{I},c}
,
Q^{s}_{\text{I},g}
,
R^{s}_{\text{I},c}
, and
R^{s}_{\text{I},g}
into a unified auxiliary sequence
Q^{s}_{\text{I}}
, which serves as an upper bound for
\lvert{\kappa^{s+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert
and
\lvert{\kappa^{s+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
, where
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
.

Clearly due to
A_{3}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}>0,
the upper bound provided by
R^{s}_{\text{I},c}
is subsumed by the upper bound provided by
Q^{s}_{\text{I},c}
and the upper bound provided by
R^{s}_{\text{I},g}
is subsumed by the upper bound provided by
Q^{s}_{\text{I},g}
. Consequently, for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
is upper bounded by
Q^{s}_{\text{I}}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

\left(Q^{s}_{\text{I},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
is expressed as

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{I}}=Q^{s}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle+(1+a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s+1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

with
Q^{0}_{\text{I},g}=(1+a_{2})\max\{{\max_{c}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}.

∎

Based on
Lemma

C.12
, we prove that
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert
for
c\neq c_{m}^{*}\text{ or }j\notin\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert
remains upper bounded throughout the trajectory by induction.

Lemma C.13
.

Consider the expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
. Let
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{c^{\prime}}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
for all
c\neq c^{\prime}
,
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
for all
c
, and
{\eta}^{s}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. For all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
, suppose that

•

\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\leq a_{2}
,

•

\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
,

•

\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
.

Then, if we have
\kappa^{t+1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\leq a_{2}
,

•

\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}
, for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
,

•

\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
,

hold with high probability.

Proof.

To begin, consider the the case when

\displaystyle(1+a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad>A_{3}\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

holds for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.

Due to Lemma
C.12
,

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{I}}\leq Q^{s}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle+(1+2a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

holds for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.

By applying
Lemma

A.4
to
Q^{s}_{\text{I}}
,

\displaystyle Q^{s}_{\text{I}}\leq\frac{Q^{0}_{\text{I}}}{{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1+3a_{2})\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}C^{-1}M^{-2}{(Q^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-2}s)}^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}

holds for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
,

where we used
\big(1+(1+2a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}\big)^{k^{*}-1}-1\leq\frac{a_{2}}{1+2a_{2}}
.

By applying
Lemma

A.4
to
P^{s}_{\text{I}}
,

\displaystyle P^{s}_{\text{I}}\geq\frac{P^{0}_{\text{I}}}{{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2}),\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}C^{-1}M^{-2}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-2}s)}^{-\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}

holds for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.

From
Corollary

C.3
,
Lemma

C.8
, and
Lemma

C.9
, it hold that
Q^{0}_{\text{I}}<P^{0}_{\text{I}}
and
(1+3a_{2})\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}\\
{(\max\{{\max_{c}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\})}^{k^{*}-2}\leq(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-2}
, which establish that
Q^{s}_{\text{I}}<P^{s}_{\text{I}}
.

Thus,
Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}\leq P^{t+1}_{\text{I}}
, which indicates that
{\max\{{\max_{c}{\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}\}}\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
.

Since
P^{s}_{\text{I}}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\leq a_{2}
,

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\eta_{e}}^{-1}{CM^{2}({(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}-{(a_{2})}^{-k^{*}+2})}}{k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{{\eta_{e}}^{-1}{C}M^{2}(1+5a_{2}){\frac{{\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}}{k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1+3a_{2}){\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}.

In contrast,
Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}>A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
holds only if

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\geq{\frac{{\eta_{e}}^{-1}CM^{2}\big({(Q^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}-{(A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}^{-k^{*}+2}\big)}{k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1+3a_{2}){\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{{\eta_{e}}^{-1}CM^{2}(1-2a_{2}){(Q^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}}{k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1+3a_{2}){\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}

where we used
{(A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}^{-k^{*}+2}\leq a_{2}(\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})^{-k^{*}+2}\leq a_{2}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}\leq a_{2}{(Q^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}
.

From
Corollary

C.3
,
Lemma

C.8
, and
Lemma

C.9
, we have

\displaystyle(1+8a_{2}){\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{\big(\max\{{\max_{c}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert},\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}\big)}^{k^{*}-2}

\displaystyle<\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}{(\kappa^{0}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}})}^{k^{*}-2}.

However, this leads to the contradiction that

\displaystyle{\eta_{e}}^{-1}CM^{2}(1-2a_{2}){(Q^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}>{\eta_{e}}^{-1}{C}M^{2}(1+5a_{2}){\frac{{\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert}},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{-k^{*}+2}.

Thus,
Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}>A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
does not hold, which implies that
Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.

Next, consider the case where

\displaystyle(1+a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad>A_{3}\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

holds for all
s=0,1,\ldots,\tau_{1}-1
, but

\displaystyle(1+a_{2})\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq A_{3}\frac{{\eta}^{s}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

holds for
s=\tau_{1}\leq t
.

Here, suppose that

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{I}}\leq Q^{s}_{\text{I}}+2a_{2}{\frac{\eta_{e}}{CM^{2}}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

holds for all
s=\tau_{1},\tau+1,\ldots,\tau_{2}{\leq t}
.

Then,

\displaystyle Q^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle=Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+\sum_{s=\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}2A_{3}\frac{\eta_{e}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+\frac{2A_{3}}{(1-a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}(P^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}}-P^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}})

\displaystyle\leq{\Big(\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}\cdot\frac{A_{3}}{(1+a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)}^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-1}}P^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2A_{3}}{(1-a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}(P^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}}-P^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}})

\displaystyle\leq{\Big(\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}\cdot\frac{A_{3}}{(1+a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)}^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-1}}P^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle(1+a_{2})\frac{\eta_{e}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}{(Q^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\leq A_{3}\frac{\eta_{e}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}{(P^{\tau_{2}+1}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

We have
Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}\leq P^{t+1}_{\text{I}}
since

\displaystyle\big(\frac{(1+a_{2})\max\{\max_{c^{\prime}}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},k^{*}}}\rvert},{\lvert{\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}{s_{c^{\prime}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{\prime}}\gamma_{k^{*}}}}\rvert}\}}{A_{3}{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}}}d^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-1}}\geq 1.

Therefore, we obtain
{\max\{{\max_{c}{\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert},\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}\}}\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
.

In the same way,

\displaystyle Q^{t+1}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle=Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+\sum_{s=\tau_{1}}^{t}2A_{3}\frac{\eta_{e}}{CM^{2}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}{(P^{s}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+\frac{2A_{3}}{(1-a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}(P^{t+1}_{\text{I}}-P^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}})

\displaystyle\quad{\leq Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+\frac{2A_{3}}{(1-a_{2})d^{\frac{1}{2}}}P^{t+1}_{\text{I}}}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}+3A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

where the last inequality is by
Q^{\tau_{1}}_{\text{I}}\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
from the first case.
∎

Finally, we establish
Lemma

C.6
.

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

Suppose that
T_{1}=\lfloor{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-5a_{2})(\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}})C^{-1}M^{-2}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}^{k^{*}-2})}^{-1}\rfloor
and
\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\leq a_{2}
,
\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}
, and
\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, where
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
for all
s=0,1,...,T_{1}
. Then the bounds given by
Lemma

C.12
and
Lemma

C.13
hold for all
s=0,1,...,T_{1}
with high probability.

Thus, by
Lemma

A.4
and
Lemma

C.12
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{t}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\geq P^{t}_{\text{I}}\geq\frac{P^{0}_{\text{I}}}{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}C^{-1}M^{-2}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}s)^{k^{*}-2}}

However, obviously when
t=T_{1}
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{t}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\geq P^{t}_{\text{I}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{P^{0}_{\text{I}}}{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}C^{-1}M^{-2}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}s)^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c^{*}_{m}}\beta_{c^{*}_{m},k^{*}}C^{-1}M^{-2})^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}>1.

This leads to a contradiction as
\kappa^{T_{1}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}\leq 1
.
Since
\max\{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert,\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\}\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
from
Lemma

C.13
,

\displaystyle\lvert{\kappa^{t_{1}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}-\kappa^{t_{1}-1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}}\rvert\leq A_{1}\eta_{e}\leq A_{1}a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(120)

Thus,

\displaystyle\lvert{\kappa^{t_{1}}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\lvert{\kappa^{t_{1}-1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert+\lvert{\kappa^{t_{1}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}-\kappa^{t_{1}-1}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}}\rvert\leq 5A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}{=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}.

(121)

∎

C.3
Router Learning Stage

This subsection is dedicated to proving that, after the exploration stage, the router successfully learns to dispatch the data
\mathbf{x}_{c}
to the appropriate experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
with high probability.

Lemma C.14
.

Take
{\eta^{t}}={\eta_{r}}\leq a_{5}d^{-1}
for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
and suppose
T_{2}\leq a_{6}d=\Theta(d)
. Then, any
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
satisfies
h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})-\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})}<0
with high probability.

To prove Lemma
C.14
, we show that, for experts not belonging to the set of professional experts, the alignment between the cluster signal and the weights of the gating network, represented as
\iota^{t}_{c,m}:={v_{c}}^{\top}{\theta^{t}_{m}}
, is upper bounded.

Lemma C.15
.

Take
{\eta^{t}}={\eta_{r}}\leq a_{5}d^{-1}
for
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
and suppose
T_{2}\leq a_{6}d=\Theta(d)
. Then, for all
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
, we have
{\iota_{c,m}^{T_{2}}}\leq-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\rho\Omega\left(\frac{1}{CJM^{3}}\right)\leq\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{\iota_{c,m^{\prime}}^{T_{2}}}-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\rho\Omega\left(\frac{1}{CJM^{3}}\right)
.

During the router learning stage, following
Chen et al. 2022
, we introduce a noise term
r_{m}
into the output of the gating network to stabilize the training of the router. Specifically, the activated expert
m(x)
is given by
m(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{arg\,max}}_{m\in[M]}\{h_{m}(x)+r_{m}\}
.

Here, we describe an important property of the softmax router: by initializing the weights of the gating network
\theta_{m}
to zero, we ensure that their sum over all experts is also zero.

Lemma C.16
.

For all
t\geq 0
, we have
\sum_{m\in[M]}\theta^{t}_{m}=\sum_{m\in[M]}\theta^{0}_{m}=0
.

Proof.

The gradient of
\theta_{m}
is formulated as

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}={(\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c})=m\right))-\pi_{m}(x_{c}))\pi_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})y_{c}f_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})x_{c}}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in[M]}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}={\sum_{m\in[M]}(\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c})=m\right))-\pi_{m}(x_{c}))\pi_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})y_{c}f_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})x_{c}=0.}

This result, combined with the initialization
\theta^{0}_{m}=0
and the fact that
\sum_{m\in[M]}\pi_{m}(x_{c})=0
, completes the proof.
∎

Following
(
Chen et al. 2022
)
, we demonstrate that the router will not route the examples to the experts with low gating network outputs.

Lemma C.17
.

Suppose the noise
\{r_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
is independently drawn from
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
. If
h_{m}(x;\Theta)\leq\max_{m^{\prime}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x;\Theta)-1
, then the example
x
will not be routed to the expert
m
.

Proof.

If
h_{m}(x;\Theta)\leq\max_{m^{\prime}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x;\Theta)-1
, then for any uniform noise
\{r_{m^{\prime}}\}_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}
, it holds that
h_{m}(x;\Theta)+r_{m}\leq\max_{m^{\prime}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x;\Theta)\leq\max_{m^{\prime}}\{h_{m^{\prime}}(x;\Theta)+r_{m^{\prime}}\}
. The first inequality follows from
r_{m}\leq 1
, and the second inequality follows from
r_{m^{\prime}}\geq 0
for all
m^{\prime}\in[M]
.
∎

In addition, following
Chen et al. 2022
, we note that when the differences in the outputs of the gating network at different time steps
t
are small, the corresponding differences in the probabilities with which the data is routed are also small.

Lemma C.18

(Lemma 5.1 of
Chen et al. 2022
)
.

Let
\mathbf{h},\hat{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
be the output of the gating network and
\{r_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}
be the noise independently drawn from
\text{Unif}[0,1]
. Denote
\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{p}}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
to be the probability that experts get routed, i.e.,
p_{m}=\mathbb{P}(\arg\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}\{h_{m^{\prime}}+r_{m^{\prime}}\}=m)
,
\hat{p}_{m}=\mathbb{P}(\arg\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}\{\hat{h}_{m^{\prime}}+r_{m^{\prime}}\}=m)
. Then we have that
\|\mathbf{p}-\hat{\mathbf{p}}\|_{\infty}\leq M^{2}\|\mathbf{h}-\hat{\mathbf{h}}\|_{\infty}
.

Based on the above properties of the gating network, we establish
Lemma

C.15
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.15
.

The population gradient for the gating network of the router can be expressed as

\displaystyle-\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}]

\displaystyle={\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}_{c}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c})=m\right)\pi_{m}(x_{c})y_{c}f_{m}(x_{c})]}

(122)

\displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{E}_{c}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[(\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c})=m\right)-\pi_{m}(x_{c}))\pi_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})y_{c}f_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})x_{c}]}.

(123)

We decompose the key components of the population gradient.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[y_{c}f_{m(x_{c})}x_{c}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{CJ}\sum_{c\in[C]}\sum_{j\in[J]}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}x_{c})+s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}x_{c})\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c})\Big)x_{c}\Big]

\displaystyle{=\frac{1}{CJ}\sum_{c\in[C]}\sum_{j\in[J]}\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}z)+s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}(z+\rho v_{c})\Big)z\Big]}

\displaystyle\quad{+\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}z)+s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}(z+\rho v_{c})\Big)\rho v_{c}\Big]\Bigg\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{CJ}\sum_{c\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\Bigg\{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{i\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i-1}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{i}\binom{i}{l}\mathrm{He}_{i-l}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}z){(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l}\Big)\Big)w^{*}_{c}\Big]}_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{i\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i-1}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{i}\binom{i}{l}\mathrm{He}_{i-l}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}z){(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l}\Big)w^{*}_{g}\Big]}_{\text{(II)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}z)+s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{i-1}\binom{i}{l}(i-l)\mathrm{He}_{i-l-1}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}z){(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l}\Big)\Big)w_{m,j}\Big]}_{\text{(III)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{z}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}z)+s_{c}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\mathrm{He}_{i}({w_{g}^{*}}^{\top}z)\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{i}\binom{i}{l}\mathrm{He}_{i-l}({w_{m,j}}^{\top}z){(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l}\Big)\Big)\rho v_{c}\Big]}_{\text{(IV)}}\Bigg\}

where the second equality follows from
x_{c}=z+\rho v_{c}
, where
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, and
{v_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{c^{\prime}}}
and
{v_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}
for all
(c,c^{\prime})
. Third equality follows from the binomial expansion, Stein’s Lemma, and integration by parts.

From now, we look at the alignment
{v_{c}}^{\top}\theta_{m}
. Thus,
(I)
and
(II)
are negligible since
{v_{c}}^{\top}w_{c^{\prime}}^{*}=0
and
{v_{c}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}=0
for all
(c,c^{\prime})
. We expand
(III)
and
(IV)
.

(III)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sum_{l=i+1}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l}{l-i-1}{(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l-i-1}\Big)\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}(i+1)!{({w_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{i}w_{m,j}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sum_{l=i+1}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l}{l-i-1}{(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l-i-1}\Big)\frac{s_{c}\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}(i+1)!{({w_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{i}w_{m,j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i+1,c}\beta_{c,i}{({\kappa_{c,m,j}})}^{i}w_{m,j}+\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sqrt{i+1}s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i+1,c}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}})}^{i}w_{m,j},

(IV)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sum_{l=i}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l}{l-i}{(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l-i}\Big)\frac{\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}i!{({w_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{i}\rho v_{c}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\sum_{l=i}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l}{l-i}{(\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c})}^{l-i}\Big)\frac{s_{c}\gamma_{i}}{\sqrt{i!}}i!{({w_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{i}\rho v_{c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{({\kappa_{c,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho v_{c}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho v_{c},

where we used the orthogonality property of Hermite polynomials in both
(III)
and
(IV)
.

We introduce the discrepancy
\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}
between the population and the empirical gradient.

\displaystyle\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}=-\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}+\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbf{E}[\mathcal{L}].

\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}
are mean-zero sub-Weibull random variables and their partial sums exhibit strong concentration behavior. In addition,
\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}
satisfies
\|{\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}\|=\tilde{O}(d^{\frac{1}{2}})}
and
\lvert{{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}}\rvert=\tilde{O}(1)
.

Next, we evaluate the empirical update of the alignment. Note that
\iota^{t}_{c,m}={v_{c}}^{\top}{\theta^{t}_{m}}
and
\pi_{m}(x_{c})=\frac{\exp({\theta_{m}}^{\top}x_{c})}{\sum_{m^{\prime}}\exp({\theta_{m^{\prime}}}^{\top}x_{c})}
.

\displaystyle{\iota_{c,m}^{t+1}}

\displaystyle={\iota_{c,m}^{t}}-{\eta^{t}}{v_{c}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle={\iota_{c,m}^{t}}+\eta^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}_{c}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c})=m\right){\pi_{m}(x_{c})y_{c}f_{m}(x_{c})}]+\eta^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}

\displaystyle={\iota_{c,m}^{t}}+\underbrace{\frac{{\eta^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}}}{CJ}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c^{\prime}}}[\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c^{\prime}})=m\right){\pi_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})y_{c^{\prime}}f_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})x_{c^{\prime}}]}}_{\text{(A)}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\frac{{\eta^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}}}{CJ}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c^{\prime}}}[\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c^{\prime}})\in\mathcal{M}_{c}\right){\pi_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})\pi_{m}(x_{c^{\prime}})y_{c^{\prime}}f_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{x^{\prime}})x_{c^{\prime}}]}}_{\text{(B)}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\frac{{\eta^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}}}{CJ}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c^{\prime}}}[\mathbf{1}\left(m(x_{c^{\prime}})\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}\right){\pi_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})\pi_{m}(x_{c^{\prime}})y_{c^{\prime}}f_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})x_{c^{\prime}}]}}_{\text{(C)}}+{\eta^{t}}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi^{t}_{\theta_{m}}.

We derive an upper bound for
\lvert\text{(A)}\rvert
and
\lvert\text{(C)}\rvert
, and a lower bound for
(B)
for
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
.
We first derive a lower bound for
\lvert\text{(A)}\rvert
and
\lvert\text{(C)}\rvert
.

\displaystyle\lvert{\text{(A)}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c^{\prime}})=m)\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big|\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{({\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m,j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m,j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c^{\prime}})=m)\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big|\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{({\kappa_{c,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho+\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}s_{c}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\eta^{t}}M\Big(p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}\rvert}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j}{\lvert{\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad+p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}\rvert}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c}}\rvert}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\eta_{r}^{t}}}{C}\Big(p^{*}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j,i,c}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}\rvert\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c,j}{\lvert{\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\rho+p^{*}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j,i,c}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}s_{c}\gamma_{i}\rvert\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}\lvert{\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\rho\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{O}({\eta_{r}}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\lvert{\text{(C)}}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c^{\prime}})=m)\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big|\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{({\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m,j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m,j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c^{\prime}})=m)\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big|\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{({\kappa_{c,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho+\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}s_{c}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\eta^{t}}M\Big(p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}\rvert}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j}{\lvert{\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad+p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}\rvert}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{\lvert{{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c}}\rvert}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{C}\Big(p^{*}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j,i,c}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}\rvert\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},c,j}{\lvert{\kappa_{c^{\prime},m,j}^{t}}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\rho+p^{*}\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j,i,c}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}s_{c}\gamma_{i}\rvert\max_{m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c},j}\lvert{\kappa_{g,m,j}^{t}\rvert}^{k^{*}}\rho\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{O}({\eta_{r}}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

where it is clear that
\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c})=m)\leq 1
and
\pi_{m}(x_{c^{\prime}}),\pi_{m(x_{c^{\prime}})}(x_{c^{\prime}})\leq 1
for all
m\in[M]
. Furthermore, by
Lemma

C.6
, all terms in the RHS are upper bounded by
\tilde{O}(\eta_{r}d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
since
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
j\in[J]
. By
Lemma

C.10
,
\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m,j,i,c}\rvert
are by at most
\tilde{O}(1)
.

Next we derive a lower bound for
(B)
.

From Lemma
C.17
, we obtain
h_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})\geq\max_{m}h_{m}(x_{c})-1
, which leads to

\displaystyle\pi_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c})=\frac{\exp(h_{m_{(x_{c})}}(x_{c}))}{\sum_{m\in[M]}\exp(h_{m(x_{c})})}\geq\frac{\exp(h_{m(x_{c})}(x_{c}))}{M\max_{m\in[M]}\exp(h_{m}(x_{c}))}\geq\frac{1}{eM}=\Omega\left(\frac{1}{M}\right).

Here, we show that
\lvert h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{t})\rvert=\lvert{\theta^{t}_{m}}^{\top}x_{c}\rvert\leq 1
for all
t=0,1,\ldots,T_{2}
.

For all
t=0,1,\ldots,T_{2}
, with high probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\theta^{t}_{m}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta^{t}_{m}-\theta^{0}_{m}\|=\eta_{r}\Big\|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}^{s}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\eta_{r}\Big\|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\Big(\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}^{s}]+\Xi^{s}_{\theta_{m}}\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{r}\Big\|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}^{s}]\Big\|+\eta_{r}\Big\|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\Xi^{s}_{\theta_{m}}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq a_{5}a_{6}A_{5}\rho+a_{5}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}\rho\leq a_{5}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}\rho.

where we used
\|\nabla_{\theta^{s}_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}]\|=\tilde{O}(1)
by
\|\nabla_{\theta^{s}_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}]\|\leq\|\mathbb{E}_{c}\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[y_{c}f_{m(x_{c})}x_{c}]\|
and the previously expanded (I), (II), (III), and (IV) along with
\|w^{*}_{c}\|=\|w^{*}_{g}\|=\|w_{m,j}\|=\|v_{c}\|=1
. Note that
\|\cdot\|
denotes
\ell_{2}
-norm.
Since
x_{c}=z+\rho v_{c}
and
{\theta_{m}^{t}}^{\top}z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\|\theta_{m}^{t}\|_{2}^{2})
, it follows that, with high probability,

\displaystyle\lvert{\theta_{m}^{t}}^{\top}z\rvert\leq a_{5}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}\rho\sqrt{\log d}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

In addition, for all
t=0,1,\ldots,T_{2}
, with high probability, we have

\displaystyle\rho|\iota^{t}_{c,m}|

\displaystyle\leq\rho|{\theta^{t}}^{\top}v_{c}-{\theta^{0}}^{\top}v_{c}|\leq\rho\eta_{r}\Big|\sum_{s=0}^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathcal{L}^{s}\Big|

\displaystyle=\rho\eta_{r}\Big|\sum_{s=0}^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}^{s}]+\sum_{s=0}^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi^{s}_{\theta_{m}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\rho\eta_{r}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\Big|{v_{c}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta_{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{L}^{s}]\Big|+\rho\eta_{r}\Big|\sum_{s=0}^{t}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi^{s}_{\theta_{m}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2}\eta_{r}T_{2}A_{5}+\rho^{2}\eta_{r}\sqrt{T_{2}}d^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}

\displaystyle\leq a_{5}a_{6}A_{5}\rho^{2}+a_{5}a_{6}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}\rho^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Thus, for all
t=0,1,\ldots,T_{2}
, we obtain that,

\displaystyle|h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{t})|

\displaystyle=|{\theta^{t}_{m}}^{\top}x_{c}|=|{\theta^{t}_{m}}^{\top}z+\rho\iota^{t}_{c,m}|

\displaystyle\leq|{\theta^{t}_{m}}^{\top}z|+\rho|\iota^{t}_{c,m}|\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}=1

with high probability.

When
|h_{m}(x_{c})|\leq 1
, it follows that
h_{m}(x_{c})\geq\max_{m^{\prime}}(h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c}))-2
for all
m\in[M]
, which implies that

\displaystyle\pi_{m}(x_{c})\geq\frac{\exp(h_{m}(x_{c}))}{M\max_{m^{\prime}}\exp(h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c}))}\geq\frac{1}{e^{2}M}=\Omega(\frac{1}{M}).

By
Lemma

C.18
, we have
\mathbb{P}(m(x_{c})\in\mathcal{M}_{c})=\Omega(\frac{1}{M})
.

Therefore,

(B)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\Omega\left(\frac{1}{M^{3}}\right)\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[C]}\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}{({\kappa_{c^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m^{\prime},j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{i+1}\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m^{\prime},j}^{t}})}^{i}({w_{m^{\prime},j}^{t}}^{\top}v_{c})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\Omega\left(\frac{1}{M^{3}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{J}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{({\kappa_{c,m^{\prime},j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho+\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c}s_{c}\gamma_{i}{({\kappa_{g,m^{\prime},j}^{t}})}^{i}\rho\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\Omega\left(\frac{1}{M^{3}}\right)\Big(\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}(\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j^{*}_{m},k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{({\kappa_{c,m^{\prime},j}^{t}})}^{k^{*}}\rho)

\displaystyle\quad-J{\lvert\mathcal{M}_{c}\rvert}p^{*}\max_{m^{\prime},j\neq j^{*}_{m}}{\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}\rvert}\max_{j\neq j^{*}_{m},m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}{(\lvert{\kappa_{c,m^{\prime},j}^{t}}\rvert)}^{k^{*}}\rho

\displaystyle\quad-J{\lvert\mathcal{M}_{c}\rvert}p^{*}\max_{m^{\prime},j,c^{\prime}}{\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c^{\prime}}\gamma_{i}\rvert}\max_{j,m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}{(\lvert{\kappa_{g,m^{\prime},j}^{t}}\rvert)}^{k^{*}}\rho

\displaystyle\quad-J{\lvert\mathcal{M}_{c}\rvert}p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{c^{\prime},m^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c^{\prime}}\beta_{c^{\prime},i}\rvert}\max_{c^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}{(\lvert{\kappa_{c^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}^{t}}\rvert)}^{k^{*}}\max_{m^{\prime},j}{\lvert{w^{t}_{m^{\prime},j}}^{\top}v_{c}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad-J{\lvert\mathcal{M}_{c}\rvert}p^{*}\sqrt{p^{*}+1}\max_{c^{\prime},m^{\prime},j,i}{\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j,i,c^{\prime}}s_{c}^{\prime}\gamma_{i}\rvert}\max_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c},j}{(\lvert{\kappa_{g^{\prime},m^{\prime},j}^{t}}\rvert)}^{k^{*}}\max_{m^{\prime},j}{\lvert{w^{t}_{m^{\prime},j}}^{\top}v_{c}\rvert}\Big)

\displaystyle={a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Omega(\frac{{\eta_{r}}\rho}{CJM^{3}})>0,

where we used that
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq a_{2}
for all
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
and
j\in\mathcal{J}_{m}^{*}
,
\tilde{\alpha}_{m^{\prime},j^{*}_{m},k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}\geq A_{6}
,

\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
for all
(c,j)\neq(c^{*}_{m},j^{*}_{m})
,
and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
for all
j\in[J]
, as shown in
Lemma

C.6
. Among the terms on the RHS of
\frac{{\eta^{t}}}{CJ}\Omega\big(\frac{1}{M^{3}}\big)\big(\cdot\big)
, all terms except for the first one are smaller than the first term by at least an order of
\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Thus, in the case where
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
, we have

\displaystyle{\iota_{c,m}^{T_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\iota_{c,m}^{T_{2}-1}}-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Theta\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)+{\eta_{r}}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi_{\theta_{m}}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\iota_{c,m}^{0}}-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\sum_{t=0}^{T_{2}}\Theta\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)+{\eta_{r}}\big\lvert{\sum_{s=0}^{T_{2}}{v_{c}}^{\top}\Xi_{\theta_{m}}^{t}}\big\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\leq-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Theta\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}T_{2}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)+\underbrace{{\eta_{r}}A_{5}\sqrt{T_{2}}}_{=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})}

\displaystyle\quad\leq-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}T_{2}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)

which holds with high probability.

Therefore, by noting from
Lemma

C.16
that

\displaystyle\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{\iota_{c,m^{\prime}}^{T_{2}}}\geq\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{\iota_{c,m^{\prime}}^{T_{2}}}=0,

we finally obtain that

\displaystyle{\iota_{c,m}^{T_{2}}}\leq-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}T_{2}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{\iota_{c,m^{\prime}}^{T_{2}}}-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{{\eta_{r}}T_{2}\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{m^{\prime}\in[M]}{\iota_{c,m^{\prime}}^{T_{2}}}-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{\rho}{CJM^{3}}\right).

∎

We establish
Lemma

C.14
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.14
.

In
Lemma

C.15
, we demonstrated that the cluster signals of clusters not assigned to the router’s gating network are aligned with high probability to be negative. Here, we aim to show that, upon observing new data
x_{c}=\rho v_{c}+z
, where
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, the data is not dispatched to the experts not assigned to the corresponding cluster with high probability.

For
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
, we have, with high probability,

\displaystyle h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})={\theta^{T_{2}}_{m}}^{\top}x_{c}=\rho\iota^{T_{2}}_{c,m}+{\theta^{T_{2}}_{m}}^{\top}z\leq a_{5}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho\underbrace{\Big(-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{6}{\rho}\Omega\left(\frac{1}{CJM^{3}}\right)+A_{5}\sqrt{\log{d}}\Big)}_{<0}<0

(124)

where we used
\lvert{\theta_{m}^{t}}^{\top}z\rvert\leq a_{5}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{5}\rho\sqrt{\log d}
with high probability and
\rho\gtrsim\frac{A_{5}\sqrt{\log{d}}}{{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}{a_{6}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{6}\Omega{(\frac{1}{CJ{M}^{3}})}}
.

By combining this result with
\sum_{m\in[M]}\theta^{T_{2}}_{m}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{m^{\prime}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}\sum_{{m^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}\sum_{{m^{\prime}}\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}}\underbrace{h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})}_{\leq-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\Omega{(\frac{{\rho}^{2}}{CJM^{3}})}}

\displaystyle\geq{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{M-|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}{|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}\frac{{\rho}^{2}}{CJM^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle>0

\displaystyle>h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})

for
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
as desired.
∎

C.4
Expert Learning Stage

In this subsection, we discuss the individual learning of experts in the context of receiving data from their assigned clusters with high probability. As in Appendix
C.2
, we introduce
A_{i}
and
a_{i}
with the following order of strength, but they do not necessarily have to be the same.

\displaystyle{A_{1}\lesssim{a_{2}}^{-1}\lesssim A_{2}\lesssim A_{4}\lesssim{a_{4}}^{-1}\lesssim A_{3}=\tilde{O}(1)}.

The proof in this subsection follows the same structure as that in Appendix
C.2
and is based on the proof by
Oko et al. 2024a
.

Re-initialization.

Before entering the expert learning stage, the expert weights
w_{mj}
are reinitialized. Although this initialization is not strictly necessary, it is performed to ensure a decent path for the alignment so that the product of
g^{*}
and the activation Hermite coefficients is positive. If the Hermite coefficients of
f^{*}
and
g^{*}
are identical, aligning with
w^{*}_{g}
may become challenging, as
w^{*}_{c}
is already aligned.

The re-initialization satisfies the following condition:

Lemma C.19

(Following Lemma2 of
Oko et al. 2024a
)
.

When
J\gtrsim\frac{1}{C}\log\frac{M}{\delta}
for each
m
in
\mathcal{M}_{c}
, we have at least
J_{\mathrm{min}}
neurons
w_{mj}
such that

\displaystyle{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}},\quad|\beta_{c,k^{*}}|{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{k^{*}-2}\geq|s_{c}\gamma_{k^{*}}|{|{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}|}^{k^{*}-2}+a_{c}{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{k^{*}-2}

(125)

with probability at least
0.999
with sufficiently large
d
, where
a_{c}
is a small constant, where
a_{c}\lesssim(\log d)^{k^{*}-2}
.

Likewise, when
J\gtrsim J_{\mathrm{min}}\,\mathrm{polylog}(d)
for each
m
in
\mathcal{M}_{c}
, we have at least
J_{\mathrm{min}}
neurons
w_{mj}
such that

\displaystyle{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}},\quad|s_{c}\gamma_{k^{*}}|{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{k^{*}-2}\geq|\beta_{c,k^{*}}|{|{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}|}^{k^{*}-2}+a_{g}{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{k^{*}-2}

(126)

with probability at least
0.999
with sufficiently large
d
, where
a_{g}
is a small constant, where
a_{g}\lesssim(\log d)^{k^{*}-2}
.

Based on re-initialization, we define the set of neurons that comparatively align with the indexed features
w^{*}_{c}
and
w^{*}_{g}
.

Definition C.20
.

We define the set
\mathcal{J}_{c}
as the set of indices
j
that satisfy the given conditions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{c}

\displaystyle:=\left\{j\in[J]\mid{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}},\quad|\beta_{c,k^{*}}|{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{k^{*}-2}\geq|s_{c}\gamma_{k^{*}}|{|{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}|}^{k^{*}-2}+a_{c}{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})}^{k^{*}-2}\right\}.

Similarly, we define the set
\mathcal{J}_{g}
as the set of indices
j
that satisfy the corresponding conditions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{g}

\displaystyle:=\left\{j\in[J]\mid{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}},\quad|s_{c}\gamma_{k^{*}}|{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{k^{*}-2}\geq|\beta_{c,k^{*}}|{|{w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c}|}^{k^{*}-2}+a_{g}{({w^{0}_{m,j}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g})}^{k^{*}-2}\right\}.

Remark C.21
.

In the rest of this section, we will discuss Phase III and IV on the event that the re-initialization was successful.

Adaptive top-
k
routing.

Importantly, in this subsection, we conduct an analysis similar to that in Appendix
C.2
, but employ a different routing strategy. As demonstrated in the previous subsection, the router does not route data to experts where
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
; however, it cannot definitively determine which expert among those where
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
should receive the data. Therefore, resolving conflicts within
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
without knowing
M_{c}
or
v_{c}
requires an alternative approach. One solution is to choose
k
experts for each
x_{c}
by the following strategy:

Expert
m
is in top-
k
if and only if
h_{m}(x)\geq 0
.

The complete MoE model, incorporating the adaptive top-
k
routing, can be expressed as
\hat{F}_{M}(x_{c};\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m=1}^{M}):=\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m}(x_{c})\geq 0\right]f_{m}(x_{c})
.
This routing strategy mitigates load imbalance, which would otherwise disrupt data routing among experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
under top-1 routing.

Lemma C.22
.

There is at least one
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
such that
f_{m}
is correctly routed with high probability over the randomness of
x_{c}
. In other words,
for some fixed
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
, on the randomness of
x_{c}
,

\mathbb{P}[\text{$x_{c}$ is routed to the set of experts including $m$}]\geq 1-d^{-A}.

(127)

In addition,
f_{m^{\prime}}
is never chosen for all
m^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
when given
x_{c}
with high probability.

Proof.

By
Lemma

C.14
, with high probability, we have

\displaystyle\max_{m^{\prime}}h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})\geq{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{M-|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}{|\mathcal{M}_{c}|}\frac{{\rho}^{2}}{CJM^{3}}\right)>0

and

\displaystyle h_{m}(x_{c};\Theta^{T_{2}})<-{a_{2}}^{k^{*}}a_{5}a_{6}A_{6}\Omega\left(\frac{{\rho}^{2}}{CJM^{3}}\right)<0

for
m\notin\mathcal{M}_{c}
.

The proof is complete, since we employ adaptive top-
k
routing, where expert
m
is activated if and only if
h_{m}(x_{c})\geq 0
.

∎

By
Lemma

C.22
, the overall MoE model is, with high probability, equivalent to
\hat{F}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}(x_{c};\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}):=\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m}(x_{c})\geq 0\right]f_{m}(x_{c})
.

Following
Oko et al. 2024a
, we sequentially demonstrate the following:

•

For
t_{3,1}\leq T_{3,1}
,
J_{c}
neurons achieve an alignment of
\tilde{\Omega}(1)
with
w^{*}_{c}
, and
J_{g}
neurons achieve an alignment of
\tilde{\Omega}(1)
with
w^{*}_{g}
, i.e., weak recovery.

•

For
t_{3,2}\leq T_{3,2}
,
J_{c}
neurons achieve an alignment of
1-\tilde{O}(1)
with
w^{*}_{c}
, and
J_{g}
neurons achieve an alignment of
1-\tilde{O}(1)
with
w^{*}_{g}
.

•

In a total time of
(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}
,
J_{c}
neurons achieve an alignment of
1-\epsilon
with
w^{*}_{c}
, and
J_{g}
neurons achieve an alignment of
1-\epsilon
with
w^{*}_{g}
, i.e., strong recovery.

Weak recovery.

In the same manner as the exploration stage, we begin by evaluating the stochastic updates for the alignments,
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}
and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}
. Subsequently, we derive the lower bound of
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
, as well as the upper bound of
{\lvert}\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}{\rvert}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
and
{\lvert}\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}{\rvert}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
. Furthermore, we demonstrate that there exists a point in time when
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
grows to a constant level, while
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
remains at the saddle point.

Lemma C.23
.

Consider the expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
and
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
. Let
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
and
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Then, with high probability, there exists some time
t_{3,1}\leq T_{3,1}=\tilde{\Theta}{({\eta_{e}}^{-1}d^{\frac{k^{*}-2}{2}})}
such that the following conditions hold:

•

{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}}\geq a_{2}
, and

•

\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq 5A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
.

The same argument applies symmetrically when exchanging
c
and
g
.

Similar to
Lemma

C.8
and
Lemma

C.9
in Appendix
C.2
, we provide a bound on the Hermite coefficients influenced by the mean vector.

Lemma C.24
.

Under Adaptive top-
k
routing and re-initialization in
Lemma

C.19
, suppose that
\lvert{v_{c}}^{\top}w^{s}_{m,j}\rvert=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
,
\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
and
\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\rvert=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t\leq\tau=\tilde{O}(d^{k^{*}-1})
. Then, by setting
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
, we obtain that
\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}^{t+1}_{m,j,i,c}-\alpha_{m,j,i}}\rvert=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
with high probability.

Proof.

Since the gradient in
Lemma

C.8
changes only by an order of
\operatorname{polylog}d
, the proof follows in the same manner.
∎

Lemma C.25
.

Consider a neuron which satisfies
\alpha_{m,j,k^{*}}\beta_{c,k^{*}}>0
and
\alpha_{m,j,i}\beta_{c,i}>0
for
k^{*}<i\leq p^{*}
. Under Adaptive top-
k
routing and re-initialization in
Lemma

C.19
, suppose that
\lvert{v_{c}}^{\top}w^{s}_{m,j}\rvert=\tilde{\Omega}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}=\tilde{\Omega}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, and
\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\rvert=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t\leq\tau=\tilde{O}(d^{k^{*}-1})
. Then, by setting
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
, we obtain that
\lvert{\tilde{\alpha}^{t+1}_{m,j,i,c}}\rvert-\lvert{\alpha_{m,j,i}}\rvert=\tilde{\Omega}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
with high probability.

Proof.

Since the gradient in
Lemma

C.9
changes only by an order of
\operatorname{polylog}d
, the proof follows in the same manner.
∎

Remark C.26
.

For the sake of conciseness in the exposition of the proof, we omit the superscript
t
in
\tilde{\alpha}^{t}_{m,j,i,c}
. Based on
Lemma

C.24
,
Lemma

C.25
, and
Lemma

C.10
, the bounds in the subsequent lemmas are properly justified, regardless of the variations in the coefficients
\tilde{\alpha}^{t}_{c,m,j,i}
.

Proof of
Lemma

C.23
.

We begin by evaluating the stochastic updates of the experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
. Since the router learns to dispatch the data
x_{c}
to the experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
with high probability by
Lemma

C.22
, we have
\mathbb{P}{(m(x_{c})\in\mathcal{M}_{c})}=1-d^{-A}
, for some
A>0
.
For clarity, we will henceforth assume and write
\mathbb{P}{(m(x_{c})=m)}=1
throughout the remainder of this proof. Thus, we consider the probability conditioned on the event that the data is routed with high probability.

By analyzing the gradient update, as in
Lemma

C.7
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{t}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{t}_{m,j}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}{a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})}]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{t})}^{2}}{2}{\|\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}{a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})}]\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{{(\eta^{t})}^{3}}{2}{\|\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}{a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})}]\|}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[\sum_{l=i}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{l\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l-1}{l-i}{(\rho{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}{v_{c}})}^{l-i}\Big)\frac{i\beta_{c,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}(i-1)!{({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{t}_{m,j})}^{i-1}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{t})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}-\frac{({\eta^{t})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+{\eta^{t}}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}-\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{t})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}-\frac{{(\eta^{t})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}},

where we introduced a mean-zero random variable
\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}=-\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})]+\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})
in the first inequality. We used similar decomposition to
Lemma

C.7
in the second inequality and adopt the notation
\frac{i\alpha_{m,j,i}}{\sqrt{i!}}+\sum_{l=i+1}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{l\alpha_{m,j,l}}{\sqrt{l!}}\binom{l-1}{l-i}{(\rho{w^{t}_{m,j}}\top v_{c^{\prime}})}^{l-i}\Big)=\frac{i{\tilde{\alpha}}^{t}_{m,j,i,c^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{i!}}
in the third inequality.

In the same way, we obtain an upper bound of
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}
.

\displaystyle\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{t}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{t}_{m,j}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)\pi_{m(x_{c})}y_{c}{a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})}]

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+{\eta^{t}}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{2})+is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}{w^{*}_{g}}-\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{t})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{({\eta^{t})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}.

Similarly, we carry out the corresponding calculations for
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}
.

\displaystyle\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{t}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}-{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}{\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}})

\displaystyle\quad+is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}}\rvert{\eta^{t}}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}-\frac{({\eta^{t})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{(\eta^{t})}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}

and

\displaystyle\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{t}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[(i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}-{\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}{\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}})

\displaystyle\quad+is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j})}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{t})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}+\frac{({\eta^{t})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta^{t}}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{t}_{m,j}}{w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}.

Next, we introduce auxiliary sequences to establish the following bounds for
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}
and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}
. The derivation follows the same approach as the proof of Lemma
C.12
.

•

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
and suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
, and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.
Then,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
is lower bounded by
P^{s}_{\text{III},c}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
, where the sequence
\left(P^{s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
is defined recursively as follows:

\displaystyle P^{0}_{\text{III},c}=(1-a_{2})\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j},\text{ and}

\displaystyle P^{s+1}_{\text{III},c}=P^{s}_{\text{III},c}+{\eta^{s}}a_{2}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*}for,c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{s}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-1}\text{ for }s\geq 0,

with high probability.

While,
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
is upper bounded by
Q^{s}_{\text{III},g}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
, where the sequence
\left(Q^{s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
is defined recursively as follows:

\displaystyle Q^{0}_{\text{III},g}=(1+a_{2})\max\left\{\lvert\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\rvert,\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{III},g}=Q^{s}_{\text{III},g}+{(1+a_{2}){\eta^{s}}}k^{*}\lvert s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}\rvert{(Q^{s}_{\text{III},g})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\text{ for }s\geq 0,

with high probability.

•

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
and suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}
, and
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.
Then,
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
is lower bounded by
P^{s}_{\text{III},g}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
, where the
\left(P^{s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
is defined recursively as follows:

\displaystyle P^{0}_{\text{III},g}=(1-a_{2})\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j},\text{ and}

\displaystyle P^{s+1}_{\text{III},g}=P^{s}_{\text{III},g}+{\eta^{s}}a_{2}k^{*}s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}{(P^{s}_{\text{III},g})}^{k^{*}-1},\text{ for }s\geq 0,

with high probability.

While,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
is upper bounded by
Q^{s}_{\text{III},c}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
, where the
\left(Q^{s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
is defined recursively as follows:

\displaystyle Q^{0}_{\text{III},c}=(1+a_{2})\max\left\{\lvert\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\rvert,\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle Q^{s+1}_{\text{III},c}=Q^{s}_{\text{III},c}+{(1+a_{2}){\eta^{s}}}k^{*}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}\rvert{(Q^{s}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\text{ for }s\geq 0,

with high probability.

Using these auxiliary sequences, we can deduce the following. The derivation is the same as that of Lemma
C.13
.

•

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
and take
\eta^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
hold for
0,1,\ldots,t
.. Then, if
\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq 4a_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with high probability.

•

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
and take
\eta^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
hold for
0,1,\ldots,t
.. Then, if
\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}\leq a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with high probability.

Suppose that
T_{3,1}=\lfloor{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-5k^{*})(\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}){(P^{0}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-2})}^{-1}\rfloor
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq a_{2}
with
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with
j\notin\mathcal{J}_{c}
for all
s=0,1,...,T_{3,1}
. Then, the above bounds holds for all
s=0,1,...,T_{3,1}
with high probability.

Thus, by
Lemma

A.4
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq P^{t}_{\text{III},c}\geq\frac{P^{0}_{\text{III},c}}{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{0}_{\text{I}})}s)^{k^{*}-2}}

However, when
t=T_{1}
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq P^{t}_{\text{III},c}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{P^{0}_{\text{III},c}}{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{0}_{\text{III},c})}s)^{k^{*}-2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-a_{2})(\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}))^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}>1.

This leads to a contradiction as
\kappa^{T_{1}}_{c,m,j}\leq 1
.
Since
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\ \leq 4A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,

\displaystyle\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}-\kappa^{t_{3,1}-1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{1}\eta_{e}\leq A_{1}a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}-1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert+\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}-\kappa^{t_{3,1}-1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq 5A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

This concludes that there exists some time
t_{3,1}\leq T_{3,1}=\Theta({\eta_{e}}^{-1}d^{\frac{k^{*}-2}{2}})
such that
\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}>a_{2}
and
\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq 5A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
with high probability.

In the same way, suppose that
T_{3,1}=\lfloor{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)(1-5k^{*})(s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}){(P^{0}_{\text{III},g})}^{k^{*}-2})}^{-1}\rfloor
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq a_{2}
with
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with
j\notin\mathcal{J}_{g}
for all
s=0,1,...,T_{3,1}
, and then, there exists some time
t_{3,1}\leq T_{3,1}=\Theta({\eta_{e}}^{-1}d^{\frac{k^{*}-2}{2}})
such that
\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{g,m,j}>a_{2}
and
\lvert{\kappa^{t_{3,1}}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq 5A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
with high probability.

∎

Transition from weak to strong recovery.

Next, we show that the neuron for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
aligns with
w^{*}_{c}
up to a large constant
1-c_{2}
. We denote
t\leftarrow t-t_{3,1}
.

Lemma C.27
.

Consider the expert
m\in{\mathcal{M}_{c}}
and
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
, where j satisfies Lemma
C.23
. Let
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
and
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Then, with high probability, there exists some time
t_{3,2}\leq T_{3,2}=\tilde{\Theta}{({\eta_{e}}^{-1})}
such that
{\kappa^{t_{3,2}}_{c,m,j}}\geq 1-a_{2}
. The same argument applies symmetrically when exchanging
c
and
g
.

Proof.

Once the alignment reaches a constant level, the projection onto the spherical constraint via
(I_{d}-w_{m,j}w_{m,j}^{\top})
weakens the signal, requiring the reconstruction of the auxiliary sequences discussed in Lemma
C.23
.

Consider experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
.

Suppose that, for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
, and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
. Then, by using a similar inequality evaluation as in
Lemma

C.7
and introducing a mean-zero random variable
\Xi^{t}_{w_{m,j}}=-\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})]+\nabla_{w_{m,j}}\mathbf{1}(m(x_{c})=m)y_{c}a_{m,j}\sigma_{m}({w^{t}_{m,j}}^{\top}x_{c}+b_{m,j})
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{s+1}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+{\eta^{s}}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}\Big[i\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\beta_{c,i}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{i-1}(1-{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad+is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert{(\eta^{s})}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d}{2}-\frac{({\eta^{s})}^{3}{A_{1}}^{3}d^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}+{\eta^{s}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{s}_{m,j}}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{s}_{w_{m,j}}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}(1-{(1-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}})}^{2})-{p^{*}}^{2}{\eta^{s}}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\quad-{p^{*}}^{2}{\eta^{s}}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}}-{(\eta^{s})}^{2}\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}{A_{1}}^{2}d+{\eta^{s}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{s}_{m,j}}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{s}_{w_{m,j}}.

Since
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\geq\frac{1}{2}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,
{p^{*}}^{2}{\eta^{s}}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}{w^{*}_{g}}}\rvert}\leq\frac{1}{4}a_{2}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}
,
{p^{*}}^{2}{\eta^{s}}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}}\leq\frac{1}{4}a_{2}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}
and
{(\eta^{s})}^{2}\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}{A_{1}}^{2}d\leq\frac{1}{4}a_{2}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}
.

For the noise term,

\displaystyle\Big\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}{\eta}^{s^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}}^{\top}){{\Xi}^{s^{\prime}}}_{w_{m,j}}}\Big\rvert\leq\begin{cases}a_{2}\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j},\quad\text{if }s\leq A_{2}d^{-k^{*}+1},\\
\frac{1}{4}a_{2}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1},\quad\text{if }s>A_{2}d^{k^{*}-1}\end{cases}

with high probability.

Therefore, by noting that
(1-{(1-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2})\leq\frac{7}{4}a_{2}
,
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
can be lower bounded as
