\displaystyle\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\geq(1-a_{2})\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}+a_{2}\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\eta^{s^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}.

By introducing an auxiliary sequence
\left(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
, where

\displaystyle P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c}=(1-a_{2})\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j},\text{ and}

\displaystyle P^{\prime s+1}_{\text{III},c}=P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}+{a_{2}{\eta^{s}}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-1}\text{ for }s\geq 0,

then
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
is lower bounded by
P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

In addition, with the same proof as
Lemma

C.23
,
by introducing an auxiliary sequence
\left(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
, where

\displaystyle Q^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},g}=6A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}},\text{ and}

\displaystyle Q^{\prime s+1}_{\text{III},g}=Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}+{(1+a_{2}){\eta^{s}}}k^{*}\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}}\rvert{(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\text{ for }s\geq 0,

then
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
is upper bounded by
Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

Similarly, for a neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
,
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
is lower bounded by
P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
and
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
is upper bounded by
Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t+1
with high probability.

\left(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
and
\left(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},g}=(1-a_{2})\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j},\text{and}

\displaystyle P^{\prime s+1}_{\text{III},g}=P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}+{a_{2}{\eta^{s}}}k^{*}s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}{(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g})}^{k^{*}-1}\text{ for }s\geq 0.

\displaystyle Q^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c}=6A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}},\text{ and}

\displaystyle Q^{\prime s+1}_{\text{III},c}=Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}+{(1+a_{2}){\eta^{s}}}k^{*}\lvert\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}\rvert{(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+A_{3}{\eta^{s}}k^{*}{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}\text{ for }s\geq 0.

Note that the periods in the notation of the auxiliary sequences
\left(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
,
\left(P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
,
\left(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},g}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
, and
\left(Q^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}\right)_{s=0}^{t+1}
are intentionally used to distinguish them from the auxiliary sequences in
Lemma

C.23
.

We prove the following arguments by induction using this auxiliary sequence, in the same manner as
Lemma

C.6
.

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
and take
\eta^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
hold for all
0,1,\ldots,t
. Then, if
\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with high probability.

In the same way,
consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
and take
\eta^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
hold for all
0,1,\ldots,t
. Then, if
\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
,
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{g,m,j}
, and
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{2}A_{3}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}
with high probability.

Consider one neuron
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
.
Suppose that
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-a_{2}
hold for all
s=0,1,\ldots,T_{3,2}
, where

\displaystyle T_{3,2}=\lfloor{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)a_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-2})}^{-1}\rfloor.

However, at
t=T_{3,2}
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq P^{\prime s}_{\text{III},c}\geq\frac{P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c}}{{(1-\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)a_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-2})s)}^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c}}{{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)a_{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}{(P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c})}^{{k^{*}-2}})}^{\frac{1}{k^{*}-2}}}>1.

This leads to contradiction. Thus, there exists some time
t_{3,2}\leq T_{3,2}
such that
\kappa^{t_{3,2}}_{c,m,j}>1-a_{2}
.
The same proof applies to
\kappa^{t_{3,2}}_{g,m,j}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
by taking
T_{3,2}=\lfloor{(\eta_{e}k^{*}(k^{*}-2)a_{2}{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\gamma_{k^{*}}}{(P^{\prime 0}_{\text{III},c})}^{k^{*}-2})}^{-1}\rfloor
.

∎

Strong recovery.

Finally, we show that the neuron for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
amplifies the alignment with
w^{*}_{c}
, and the neuron for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
amplifies the alignment with
w^{*}_{g}
, and we establish strong recovery (
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-\epsilon>1-a_{2}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
, and
\kappa^{t}_{g,m,j}\geq 1-\epsilon>1-a_{2}
for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{g}
).

Lemma C.28
.

Consider the expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
and
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
, where j satisfies
Lemma

C.27
. Let
{w_{c}^{*}}^{\top}w_{g}^{*}\leq A_{4}d^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\tilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
,
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e^{\prime}}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
for
0\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})-1
and
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e^{\prime}}\leq\min\{\frac{a_{4}}{3}\epsilon d^{-1},\frac{a_{4}}{9}{\epsilon}^{2}\}
for
(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}-1
. Then,
\kappa^{(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}}_{c^{*}_{m},m,j^{*}_{m}}>1-\epsilon
where
T_{3,3}=\tilde{\Theta}({\epsilon}^{-1}{\eta_{e^{\prime}}}^{-1})
holds with high probability.

The same argument applies symmetrically when exchanging
c
and
g
.

Proof.

Consider experts
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
.
Suppose that, for
j\in\mathcal{J}_{c}
, we have
1-2a_{2}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\kappa^{s+1}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2}}){(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{s}\sum_{i=k^{*}}^{p^{*}}is_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}{(\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})}^{i-1}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j})-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}{\eta_{e^{\prime}}}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d+\eta_{e^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{s}_{w_{m,j}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2}}){(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\quad-\eta_{e^{\prime}}{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}}\rvert}(1-{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad-\eta_{e^{\prime}}{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{*}_{c}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top})w^{s}_{m,j}\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}{\eta_{e^{\prime}}}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d+\eta_{e^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s}_{m,j}{w^{s}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{s}_{w_{m,j}}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\frac{6}{5}\eta^{s^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j}})+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\eta_{e^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}}^{\top})\Xi^{s^{\prime}}_{w_{m,j}}.

Since
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-3a_{2}
, we have
{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{*}_{c}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top})w^{s}_{m,j}\leq\sqrt{6a_{2}}
and
\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}=({w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}w^{*}_{c})({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{s}_{m,j})+{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{*}_{c}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top})w^{s}_{m,j}\leq\sqrt{6a_{2}}+\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. Hence, we obtain
\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{({\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}}^{2})}){(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{k^{*}-1}\geq\frac{9}{5}\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}})
by
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-3a_{2}
,
\eta_{e^{\prime}}{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha_{m,j,i,c}}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{\lvert{{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}}\rvert}(1-{(\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j})}^{2})\leq\frac{1}{5}\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}})
by
{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}w^{*}_{g}=\tilde{O}(d^{-\frac{1}{2}})
,
\eta_{e^{\prime}}{p^{*}}^{2}\max_{i}{\lvert{s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}}\rvert}{\lvert{\kappa^{s}_{g,m,j}}\rvert}^{k^{*}-1}{w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}(I_{d}-w^{*}_{c}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top})w^{s}_{m,j}\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq\frac{1}{5}\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}})
by
{\max_{i}{\lvert s_{c}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,i,c}\gamma_{i}\rvert}/\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{2}}}
when
\alpha_{m,j,i},\,\rho{w_{m,j}}^{\top}v_{c}>0
, and
\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}{\eta_{e^{\prime}}}^{2}{A_{1}}^{2}d\leq\frac{1}{5}\eta^{s}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}})
by
\eta_{e^{\prime}}\leq\frac{a_{4}}{3}\epsilon d^{-1}
. In addition, we have
\lvert{\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\eta_{e^{\prime}}{w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}(I_{d}-{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}}^{\top}{w^{s^{\prime}}_{m,j}})\Xi^{s^{\prime}}_{w_{m,j}}}\rvert\leq a_{2}\epsilon+\frac{1}{5}\eta_{e^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-{\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j}})
with high probability.

Therefore, if
1-3a_{2}\leq\kappa^{s}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
, for all
s=0,1,\ldots,t
,

\displaystyle\kappa^{s+1}_{c,m,j}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}-\frac{a_{2}}{3}\epsilon+\sum_{s^{\prime}=0}^{s}\eta_{e^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}(1-\kappa^{s^{\prime}}_{c,m,j})

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{0}_{c,m,j}-\frac{a_{2}}{3}\epsilon+\frac{1}{3}s\eta_{e^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}\epsilon.

and
1-3a_{2}\leq\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}
hold.

For
0\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})-1
, it holds that
\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}-\frac{\epsilon}{3}\geq 1-2a_{2}
by taking
\eta^{t}=\eta_{e}\leq a_{4}d^{-\frac{k^{*}}{2}}
. Take
{\eta}^{t}=\eta_{e^{\prime}}\leq\min\{\frac{a_{4}}{3}\epsilon d^{-1},\frac{a_{4}}{9}{\epsilon}^{2}\}
and suppose that
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\leq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
and for all
(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}-1
, where

\displaystyle T_{3,3}=\lfloor{a_{2}{(\eta_{e^{\prime}}\epsilon k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}})}^{-1}}\rfloor+1.

Then, it holds that
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-3a_{2}+\frac{1}{3}t\epsilon\eta_{e^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}
for all
(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}-1
. However, at
t=(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}
,
1-3a_{2}+\frac{1}{3}t\epsilon\eta_{e^{\prime}}k^{*}\tilde{\alpha}_{m,j,k^{*},c}\beta_{c,k^{*}}\geq 1
, which leads to contradiction. Thus, there exists some
t_{3,3}\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}
such that
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
.
When there exists some time
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}<1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
for
t>t_{3,3}
,
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}\geq 1-2a_{2}
since
\lvert{\kappa^{t+1}_{c,m,j}-\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}}\rvert\leq A_{1}\eta_{e^{\prime}}
. Thus,
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}>1-\epsilon
holds for all
t_{3,3}\leq t\leq(T_{3,1}-t_{3,1})+(T_{3,2}-t_{3,2})+T_{3,3}
until
\kappa^{t}_{c,m,j}>1-\frac{\epsilon}{3}
holds. By recursively applying this step, we obtain the desired result.
∎

C.5
Second Layer Optimization Stage

We have i.i.d. test-time inputs
X^{T}=(x^{1},\dots,x^{T})
and we extract
T_{c}
inputs
X_{c}^{T_{c}}=(x_{c}^{1},\dots,x_{c}^{T_{c}})
in the cluster
c
from
X^{T}
. Note that
\sum_{c}T_{c}=T
. We know that each
X_{c}^{T_{c}}
is successfully routed to the expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
with high probability over the randomness of
X^{T}
.

C.5.1
Approximation of Single Index Polynomials

We suppose
\sigma_{m}
are ReLU functions.

Lemma C.29

(Following
Damian et al. 2022
;
Oko et al. 2024a
)
.

Fix
c
and the corresponding expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
such that for all
t_{c}
,
h_{m}(x^{t_{c}})\geq 0
with high probability.
Suppose that
b_{j}\sim\mathrm{Unif}([-C_{b},C_{b}])
with
C_{b}=\tilde{O}(1)
. Let
h_{c}(z)
be a polynomial with degree
q=O(1)
,
w\in\operatorname{Unif}{(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}(1))}
,
w^{-}=-w
. Then there exists
a_{1},\dots,a_{2N}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\underset{t_{c}=1,\dots,T_{c}}{\sup}\left|\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{j}\sigma_{m}(w^{\top}x^{t_{c}}_{c}+b_{j})-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{j}\sigma_{m}({w^{-}}^{\top}x^{t_{c}}_{c}+b_{j})-h_{c}(w^{\top}x^{t_{c}}_{c})\right|=\tilde{O}(N^{-1}).

(128)

Moreover, we have
\sum_{j=1}^{2N}a_{j}^{2}=\tilde{O}(N)
and
\sum_{j=1}^{2N}|a_{j}|=\tilde{O}(N)
.

We obtain similar approximation results for polynomial activations (see
(
Oko et al. 2024a
)
for details).
Using
Lemma

C.29
, we show that ,for all
c
, there exists some
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
and
a^{*}_{m}
such that
f_{m}
can approximate
f^{*}_{c}+s_{c}g^{*}
.

Lemma C.30

(Following
Oko et al. 2024a
)
.

Let
\sigma_{m}
,
m=1,\dots,M
be ReLU activations or polynomial activations.
Fix
c
and the corresponding expert
m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
such that for all
t_{c}
,
h_{m}(x^{t_{c}})\geq 0
with high probability.
Assume
J\gtrsim J_{\mathrm{min}}\mathrm{poly}\log d
.
There exists some parameters
a^{*}_{m}=(a^{*}_{mj})_{j}
such that

\frac{1}{T_{c}}\sum_{t_{c}}\left(\sum_{m^{\prime}=1}^{M}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m^{\prime}}(x_{c}^{t_{c}})\geq 0\right]}{J}\sum_{j}a_{m^{\prime}j}^{*}\sigma_{m^{\prime}}(\hat{w}_{m^{\prime}j}^{\top}x_{c}^{t_{c}}+b_{j})-f_{c}^{*}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}x_{c}^{t_{c}})-s_{c}g^{*}({w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}x_{c}^{t_{c}})\right)^{2}\lesssim\tilde{O}(|J_{\mathrm{min}}|^{-2}+\epsilon^{2}).

(129)

where
\|a^{*}_{m}\|^{2}_{2}=\tilde{O}(J^{2}|J_{\mathrm{min}}|^{-1})
,
\|a^{*}_{m}\|_{1}=\tilde{O}(J\sqrt{C})
and
a^{*}_{m^{\prime}j}=0
for
m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}\setminus\{m\}
.

Proof.

The main difference between the proof in
(
Oko et al. 2024a
)
is that we may have superfluous experts
m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}\setminus\{m\}
. However, we only need to put
a^{*}_{m^{\prime}j}=0
for all
m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}\setminus\{m\}
and
j=1,\dots,J
.
∎

C.5.2
Optimizing the Second Layer

We present the result of optimization of the second layer:

Lemma C.31
.

Suppose that
J=\Theta(J_{\mathrm{min}}\mathrm{poly}\log d)
.
There exists
\lambda>0
such that the ridge estimator
\hat{a}_{m}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{x_{c},c}\left[\left|\sum_{m}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m}(x_{c}^{t_{c}})\geq 0\right]f_{m,\hat{a}_{m}}(x_{c})-f_{c}^{*}({w^{*}_{c}}^{\top}x_{c})-s_{c}g^{*}({w^{*}_{g}}^{\top}x_{c})\right|\right]\lesssim\tilde{O}\left((|J_{\mathrm{min}}|^{-1}+\epsilon)+\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{poly}\log d}{T}}\right).

(130)

with probability at least
1-o_{d}(1)
. Therefore, by taking
|J_{\mathrm{min}}|=\tilde{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
and
T=\tilde{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
, we have
\tilde{O}(\epsilon)
loss.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\mathcal{M}}=\{\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\mid\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|\hat{a}_{m}\|_{r}\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}}\|a^{*}_{m}\|_{r}\}
.
We know that the router
h
exclusively route
X_{c}^{T_{c}}
to the subset of experts
\subset\mathcal{M}_{c}
with high probability. Therefore, the minimization problem of the empirical
L^{2}
loss is decomposed as

\displaystyle\underset{\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\{1,\dots,M\}}}{\mathrm{min}}\;\;\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{M}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m}(x^{t})\geq 0\right]f_{m,\hat{a}_{m}}(x^{t})-y^{t}\right)^{2}

(131)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\{1,\dots,M\}}}{\mathrm{min}}\;\;\sum_{c}\frac{T_{c}}{T}\left[\frac{1}{T_{c}}\sum_{t_{c}}\left(\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m^{\prime}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})\geq 0\right]f_{m^{\prime},\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})-y^{t_{c}}_{c}\right)^{2}\right]

(132)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{\otimes_{c}\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}},\;\text{where}\;\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}}{\mathrm{min}}\;\;\sum_{c}\frac{T_{c}}{T}\left[\frac{1}{T_{c}}\sum_{t_{c}}\left(\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m^{\prime}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})\geq 0\right]f_{m^{\prime},\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})-y^{t_{c}}_{c}\right)^{2}\right]

(133)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{c}\frac{T_{c}}{T}\;\underset{\{\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}\}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}}{\mathrm{min}}\;\;\underbrace{\left[\frac{1}{T_{c}}\sum_{t_{c}}\left(\underbrace{\sum_{m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}\mathbbm{1}\left[h_{m^{\prime}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})\geq 0\right]f_{m^{\prime},\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})}_{\eqqcolon\hat{f}_{c}}-y^{t_{c}}_{c}\right)^{2}\right]}_{\eqqcolon\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{c}}[\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}]},

(134)

where we used
h_{m^{\prime}}(x^{t_{c}}_{c})\geq 0\Rightarrow m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}
with high probability,
\mathcal{M}_{c}\cap\mathcal{M}_{c^{\prime}}=\emptyset
for all
c\neq c^{\prime}
, and
\forall c,\,\{\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}\}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}\Rightarrow\{\hat{a}_{m}\}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\{1,\dots,M\}}
. Therefore, the optimization is performed in parallel for each subset of parameters
\{\hat{a}_{m},m\in\mathcal{M}_{c}\}
and we can bound the population loss as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c}[\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[|\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}|]]

(135)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{c}\frac{1}{C}\;\left(\underset{\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\mathcal{M}_{c}}}{\sup}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[|\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}|]-\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{c}}[|\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}|]\right\}+\sqrt{\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{c}}[(\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c})^{2}]}\right).

(136)

Applying Lemma 14 of
(
Oko et al. 2024a
)
, we have

\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{c}}[(\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c})^{2}]=\tilde{O}\left(\underbrace{|J_{\mathrm{min}}|^{-1}+\epsilon}_{\text{Approximation}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T_{c}}}}_{\text{Concentration}}\right)

(137)

and the generalization error is bounded as

\underset{\hat{a}_{m^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{A}^{*}_{\{m^{\prime}\}},\;m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{c}}{\sup}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{x_{c}}[|\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}|]-\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{c}}[|\hat{f}_{c}-y_{c}|]\right\}\leq\tilde{O}\left(|J_{\mathrm{min}}|^{-1}+\epsilon+\frac{\mathrm{poly}\log d}{\sqrt{T_{c}}}\right)

(138)

with probability at least
1-o_{d}(1)
for the ridge estimator.
∎
